Tender includes substantial scope relating to 
probable deployment of new telecom shelters, 
upgradation of existing shelters, passive 
infrastructure integration and long-term O&M 
responsibilities for shelter infrastructure. The 
project also includes operation & maintenance of 
more than 400 existing GFGNL shelters. Tender 
further mandates OEM Authorization / MAF, 
warranty support, spare availability and lifecycle 
support obligations for various active and passive 
telecom products. However, no specific mandatory 
Shelter OEM Authorization requirement has been 
prescribed despite telecom shelters being a critical 
telecom infrastructure asset. 
It is requested to kindly incorporate 
mandatory submission of valid 
Manufacturer Authorization Form (MAF) 
from the telecom Shelter OEM's. 
Telecom shelter infrastructure directly 
impacts network uptime, equipment 
protection, environmental control, 
power reliability, maintainability and 
long-term O&M performance of 
BharatNet infrastructure. Further, 
considering that the project includes 
long-term operation & maintenance 
responsibilities for more than 400 
existing GFGNL shelters  which are 
exclusive property of GFGNL along with 
additional proposed shelter 
infrastructure and associated shelter 
equipments for a period of 10 years. 
Considering stringent network uptime 
obligations at Block level(which is 
currently maintained at 99% being the 
best in industry), it becomes crucial to 
obtain OEM authorization and support 
from technically qualified and 
experienced Shelter OEMs having long-
term support capability. It is further 
requested that: (i) the proposed Shelter 
OEM should possess proven experience 
of minimum 100+ telecom 
shelters/porta cabins(as per the clause 
no 4.73 point no xii of the RFP) involving 
design, supply, installation, testing & 
commissioning and/or maintenance, (ii) 
the Shelter OEM should provide 
undertaking towards warranty support, 
spare availability, lifecycle support, after-
sales support and structural support for 
the complete contract duration, (iii) the 
Pl see updated 
Clause in 
corrigendum-III 
with annexure-
XX(MAF Required 
list)

Shelter OEM should declare make/model 
of major shelter equipments like BB, AC, 
SMPS at bid stage, and (iv) only 
technically qualified and OEM-backed 
shelter vendors should be permitted for 
execution of shelter-related works to 
ensure uniform quality standards, OEM 
accountability and long-term reliability 
of BharatNet shelter infrastructure.

Clause 5.12: 
Page 154 
onwards; 
Clause 4.73: 
Page 111; 
relevant O&M 
/ MAF clauses 
Clause 5.12 covers Scope of Work for BSNL 
Exchange / Shelter, including shelter-related 
deployment, upgradation and O&M activities. 
Clause 4.73 permits subcontracting of 
infrastructure-related activities. The project also 
involves long-term O&M of existing BharatNet 
shelter infrastructure, including more than 400 
existing shelters, and probable 
deployment/upgradation of additional shelters. 
Shelter environments require controlled operating 
conditions for telecom and power equipment 
through continuous functioning of air-
conditioning/cooling infrastructure. 
It is submitted that telecom shelter 
uptime and operational reliability are 
substantially dependent on proper 
cooling performance, thermal load 
management, air circulation design, 
insulation quality, heat dissipation 
planning and integration of air-
conditioning systems within the shelter 
environment. Further, shelter air-
conditioning systems should support 
integration with shelter 
alarm/monitoring systems for 
temperature alarms, fault indications 
and operational monitoring during 
network O&M operations. Any mismatch 
between shelter configuration and 
cooling system design may adversely 
affect telecom equipment performance, 
energy efficiency, equipment life and 
overall network uptime during long-term 
O&M operations. Therefore, considering 
that the project includes approximately 
10 years O&M responsibilities, more 
than 400 existing BharatNet shelters, 
probable installation/upgradation of 
additional shelters, and stringent uptime 
obligations, it is requested that 
mandatory Manufacturer Authorization 
Form (MAF) from the proposed Shelter 
OEM may kindly be incorporated in the 
tender. It is further requested that the 
Shelter OEM MAF should clearly specify 
the proposed shelter make/model along 
Please see 
preceding Response 
in query Sr. Nu. 10

Requested Amendment: 
We request the authority to include a Disaster 
Recovery (DR) site along with the necessary 
compute, storage, network, security, replication, 
backup, and DR management components to 
ensure business continuity and high availability of 
critical services. 
The proposed project includes critical IT 
infrastructure components such as 
SNOC, Data Center, Servers, Storage 
Systems, Network, and Security solutions 
that are expected to support mission-
critical applications and citizen services. 
In the absence of a Disaster Recovery 
(DR) setup, any unforeseen event such 
as hardware failure, cyberattack, 
ransomware incident, natural disaster, 
power outage, or site-level disruption 
may lead to prolonged service downtime 
and potential data loss. 
A DR infrastructure is considered an 
industry best practice for ensuring 
Business Continuity (BC) and Disaster 
Recovery (DR) compliance. It enables 
rapid restoration of services, minimizes 
Recovery Time Objective (RTO) and 
Recovery Point Objective (RPO), and 
ensures uninterrupted delivery of critical 
services to stakeholders. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

operational processes. 
 
Combining both responsibilities under a 
single agency may lead to the following 
challenges: 
 
Difficulty in identifying the root cause of 
SLA violations. 
Delays in fault resolution due to 
dependency between field fiber teams 
and NOC teams. 
Increased operational complexity and 
accountability issues. 
Limited participation of specialized 
network management and NOC 
operators. 
Risk of compromised service quality due 
to diverse and highly specialized scope 
requirements. 
Challenges in maintaining stringent 
uptime commitments across a statewide 
network. 
 
Industry best practices for large telecom 
and broadband projects generally 
separate passive infrastructure 
management from centralized NOC/SOC 
operations, enabling clear accountability, 
specialized expertise, and better SLA 
governance. 
 
Therefore, it is requested that the 
authority consider separate 
implementation packages or permit 
specialized consortium partners with 
Pls refer revision for 
S-NOC 
implementation 
timeline. 


Page 58 of 412 
 
clearly defined SLA ownership for OFC 
infrastructure and S-NOC operations. 
This approach will improve operational 
efficiency, ensure better service quality, 
enhance accountability, and facilitate 
successful project execution throughout 
the contract period.

3 -

y 
Criteria 
3.1 PRE-
QUALIFICATIO
N CRITERIA  
10. An ISP/ TSP license holder for Gujarat LSA shall 
be ineligible to participate.  
The intent of this clause appears to be to 
prevent participation of entities that 
directly compete with BSNL as Telecom 
Service Providers (TSPs). However, the 
present tender has been floated by an 
SPV and is not in direct competition with 
TSPs or ISPs. As such, the clause 
unnecessarily restricts participation of 
major Public Sector Undertakings and 
telecom infrastructure providers such as 
RailTel and other established telecom 
entities, thereby limiting competition 
and reducing the pool of qualified 
bidders. 
 
Our Ask: 
Removal if ISP/TSP Cluase for healthy 
competion. 
OR 
It is requested that the restriction, if 
considered necessary, be applicable only 
to the Lead Bidder. Further, the Lead 
Bidder should be permitted to utilize the 
technical and financial credentials of 
consortium membe having ISP/TSP 
Licennse in Gujarat LSA), subject to an 
undertaking that neither the Lead Bidder 
nor the consortium members shall 
undertake ISP-related services within the 
State of Gujarat during the period of the 
contract, as may be required under the 
tender conditions. 
No Change. The 
provision shall 
remain as specified 
in the RFP, as it is 
based on 
requirements 
mandated by DBN 
at the national level 
under the ABP 
framework.

3.1 PRE-
QUALIFICATIO
N CRITERIA  
The tender stipulates that a bidder should not 
have, directly or indirectly, more than 50% 
ownership, shareholding, management control, or 
beneficial interest in an organization holding a 
TSP/ISP License in Gujarat LSA. 
The intent of the provision appears to be 
limited to preventing participation by 
entities having more than 50% direct or 
indirect stake/control in a TSP/ISP 
licensee operating in Gujarat LSA. 
Therefore, where the bidder itself is not 
a TSP/ISP license holder, the presence of 
such a license with a subsidiary, 
associate, or group company should not 
be construed as a disqualification unless 
the bidder falls within the specific >50% 
ownership/control criteria set out in the 
tender. 
 
Kindly confirm that this interpretation is 
correct. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

positive 470 Cr at the close of the preceding 
financial year or subsequent to that.

Kindly reconsider the Technical

As per the BSNL Master RFP, a bidder 
participating in multiple packages 
across Category-1 and Category-2 is 
required to demonstrate cumulative 
EPC OFC network construction 
experience ranging from 8,000 Km to a 
maximum of 12,000 Km, even for 
participation in up to four packages. 
 
However, for the present GFGNL tender, 
which consists of a single package, the 
eligibility criteria require experience is : 
• Three orders, each having 12,000 Km 
OFC construction or ₹500 Crore value; or 
• Two orders, each having 15,000 Km 
OFC construction or ₹600 Crore value; or 
• One order having 25,000 Km OFC 
construction or ₹1,000 Crore value. 
 
The current eligibility requirement 
appears significantly more stringent than 
the corresponding criteria prescribed 
under the BSNL Master RFP, despite the 
latter covering multiple packages of a 
substantially larger scope. As presently 
defined, the criteria may limit 
participation to only a few large 
bidders, thereby restricting competition 
and reducing broader industry 
participation. 
 
Therefore, we request the Purchaser to 
rationalize the Technical Capability 
criteria by allowing cumulative OFC 
The relevant clause 
has been revised.

requested to kindly 
refer to the 
Corrigendum/Maste
r RFP for the 
updated provision

construction experience and reducing

Year Kms: If O&M of “X” Kms of OFC has been 
done for “N” years (N>=1), then ‘Year Kms’=” X

Kindly reconsider and rationalize the 
Operation & Maintenance (O&M) 
eligibility criteria and align the same with 
the provisions of the BSNL Master RFP. 
 
As per the BSNL Master RFP, the O&M 
experience requirement is linked to the 
EPC Network Construction capability 
requirement. Accordingly, a bidder 
participating in one Category-1 package 
is required to demonstrate O&M 
experience corresponding to 8,000 Year-
Kms, while the maximum requirement 
for participation across multiple 
packages is limited to 12,000 Year-Kms. 
 
However, under the present GFGNL 
tender, which consists of a single 
package, bidders are required to have 
satisfactorily completed O&M of OFC 
networks for at least 85,000 Year-Kms, 
with a minimum of 40% of the 
requirement to be met through O&M of 
24-core or higher aerial fibre cable 
networks deployed under BharatNet, 
State SPVs, ADSS, or OPGW networks. 
 
The prescribed requirement of 85,000 
Year-Kms for a single package appears 
significantly higher than the 
corresponding requirements under the 
BSNL Master RFP, which covers multiple 
packages of substantially larger scope. 
The current criterion may limit 
participation to only a few large bidders, 
thereby restricting competition and 
reducing wider industry participation. 
 
The relevant clause 
has been revised. 
Bidders are 
requested to kindly 
refer to the 
Corrigendum/Maste
r RFP for the 
updated provision

and align it with the BSNL Master RFP by

intended quality and operational 
standards.

SECTION
-7  
SLA & Penalty 
Framework 
GFGNL introduces highly granular SLA with 
multiple KPIs (uptime, latency, MTTR, packet loss, 
manpower availability, event-based penalties, etc.) 
 Kindly clarify whether all SLA-related 
KPIs are required to be integrated with 
the BSNL NOC as well. 
 
As per the BSNL Master RFP, the NOC 
monitors a defined set of critical and 
limited KPIs to ensure effective network 
monitoring and operational efficiency. 
Therefore, for smoother 
implementation and operational 
alignment, we request GFGNL to 
consider adopting a KPI framework

1

Duct/DI
T 
related 
clause - 
page 
141 
DIT (Duct 
Integrity Test) 
Requirement 
Following the completion of Trenching and Ducting 
(T&D), the Duct Integrity Test (DIT) should be 
performed prior to cable blowing to ensure system 
readiness and avoid future challenges 
 It is requested to kindly remove the 
requirement for DIT (Duct Integrity Test) 
from the project scope. 
 
DIT is generally conducted for 
spare/empty ducts prior to cable 
installation to verify duct integrity and 
ensure suitability for cable blowing 
activities. In the present project, the 
scope primarily involves upgradation, 
rehabilitation, augmentation, and limited 
new creation of the existing passive 
network infrastructure. 
 
Further, for newly created routes, the 
optical fiber cable shall be blown into the 
duct immediately after duct installation. 
Since the duct will not remain spare or 
unused for any significant period, 
conducting a separate DIT would not 
provide any additional technical benefit. 
The successful completion of cable 
blowing itself shall demonstrate the 
integrity and continuity of the duct path. 
 
Accordingly, we request GFGNL to 
exclude the DIT requirement from the 
project scope, as the requirement is not 
technically relevant for the proposed 
implementation methodology and may 
result in additional time and cost 
without corresponding value. 
Pls see revision. DIT 
(Duct Integrity Test) 
removed from the 
scope. However, 
PIA has to ensure 
highest quality 
standard and 
process 
framework   implem
ented during ABP 
project.

As per aforementioned guidelines there shall be 
three categories for manufacturers/ suppliers: 
I. 
Class-I Local Supplier: A supplier or service provider 
whose goods, services or works offered for 
procurement meets minimum ‘Local Content’ of 
50%. 
II. 
Class-II Local Supplier: A supplier or service 
provider whose goods, services and works offered 
for procurement meets minimum ‘Local Content’ 
of 20% but less than 50%. 
III. 
Non-Local Supplier: A supplier or service provider 
whose goods, services and works offered for 
procurement has ‘Local Content’ less than 20%. 
Please confirm whether the LC is to be 
submitted against the total contract 
value (Supply + Services) or only against 
the Supply value. If applicable only to the 
Supply value, please clarify whether the 
cumulative LC value should be 
maintained at a minimum of 50% of the 
Supply Order value.

requirements shall 
be applicable on the

supply, services, 
and works), in line 
with the Public

in India) guidelines.

(Gram Panchayat) locations, the utility 
power supply is often unstable and 
subject to frequent interruptions and 
voltage fluctuations, which may 
adversely affect the charging process. 
 
Since the system is required to provide 6 
hours of backup at full load, a longer 
recharge duration is essential to ensure 
complete battery charging and reliable 
operation under field conditions. 
 
Therefore, we request you to kindly 
revise the battery recharge time 
specification from 6–8 hours to 8–10 
hours, which would be more practical 
and suitable for actual site conditions 
without affecting system performance 
and availability. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

3.1 PRE-
QUALIFI
CATION 
CRITERI
A 
3. Experience 
Offered Product (OEM) NMS  Experience: 
Deployment and  maintaining of NMS including  
Helpdesk-Ticketing solution as a  bundle, with 
Minimum 3 projects  comprising of both  each 
catering to managing  minimum 12,000 network 
devices during last  7 years 
OR  
Minimum 2 projects each catering to minimum 
15,000 devices during last 7 years  
Or  
Minimum 1 projects each catering to minimum 
24,000 devices during last 7 years  
  
Definition: Network devices will be same as 
mentioned above in active devices experiences. 
In similar large-scale BharatNet/S-NOC 
implementations, such qualification 
criteria were not prescribed. We 
appreciate the inclusion of prior 
deployment experience criteria in the 
present RFP to ensure selection of a 
proven and capable OEM. However, the 
current eligibility criteria may limit 
participation of otherwise qualified and 
proven OEMs. We kindly request 
consideration of the following: 
 
• The project-wise slab criteria (3 
projects of 12,000 devices / 2 projects of 
15,000 devices / 1 project of 24,000 
devices) restricts participation of proven 
OEMs. The objective should be to assess 
the OEM's overall capability and 
experience in managing large-scale 
network environments, which may be 
demonstrated through cumulative 
deployments across multiple projects. 
Accordingly, we kindly request that the 
experience criteria be considered based 
on managing a cumulative minimum of 
50,000 active network devices across 
one or more eligible projects, including 
NMS and Helpdesk/Ticketing solutions. 
 
• The definition of network devices 
based on specific device categories is 
also restrictive. In large-scale 
deployments, the monitored 
infrastructure typically comprises 
heterogeneous devices across multiple 
technology domains, and the same 
device categories may not necessarily be 
present in every project. Accordingly, 
The relevant clause 
has been revised. 
Bidders are 
requested to kindly 
refer to the 
Corrigendum/Maste
r RFP for the 
updated provision 


Page 145 of 412 
 
qualification may be considered based 
on the total number of active monitored 
devices rather than specific device types. 
 
So, we kindly request to relax and amend 
the clause as below: 
 
"Offered Product (OEM) NMS 
Experience: Deployment and 
maintenance of NMS including Helpdesk-
Ticketing solution as a bundle, with 
experience of monitoring and managing 
a cumulative minimum of 50,000+ active 
network devices across one or more 
projects executed during the last 7 years. 
Reference PO copy and completion/sign-
off document need to be submitted. 
 
Definition: Network devices shall include 
all active devices being monitored and 
managed through the proposed 
solution."

only two MAFs ( Primary and seconday)

limiting the supply options for bidder to

long-term O&M of telecom shelter infrastructure 
including more than 400 existing BharatNet 
shelters along with associated telecom power 
systems and environmental support infrastructure. 
It is submitted that for proper 
interoperability, maintainability and 
long-term O&M support of telecom 
shelter infrastructure, the proposed 
Shelter OEM declaration/MAF should 
clearly specify the Battery Bank and 
SMPS make/model details proposed 
under the project. Telecom SMPS and 
Battery Bank systems deployed in 
telecom shelters generally require 
proper compatibility and coordination 
with shelter infrastructure for ensuring 
charging efficiency, thermal stability, 
load handling, maintenance accessibility, 
cable routing and long-term backup 
performance during network operations. 
Further, telecom power systems are 
normally integrated with 
monitoring/alarm systems for rectifier 
faults, battery health, mains failure, 
temperature alerts and DC system 
performance during long-term O&M 
operations. Considering that the project 
involves approximately 10 years O&M 
responsibilities together with stringent 
network uptime obligations, use of 
technically proven and reputed SMPS 
systems becomes important for better 
shelter efficiency, stable DC 
performance, alarm integration 
reliability and long-term operation of 
telecom shelter infrastructure. In this 
regard, kindly clarify whether Shelter

Point No. 28 
        UPS for GP  - Recharge time -6-8 hours 
We wish to submit that the specified 
recharge time of 6–8 hours for Li-ion 
batteries is achievable under ideal 
operating conditions. However, at GP 
(Gram Panchayat) locations, the utility 
power supply is often unstable and 
subject to frequent interruptions and 
voltage fluctuations, which may 
adversely affect the charging process. 
 
Since the system is required to provide 6 
hours of backup at full load, a longer 
recharge duration is essential to ensure 
complete battery charging and reliable 
operation under field conditions. 
 
Therefore, we request you to kindly 
revise the battery recharge time 
specification from 6–8 hours to 8–10 
hours, which would be more practical 
and suitable for actual site conditions 
without affecting system performance 
and availability. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

The switch shall have minimum 512 IPv4 Unicast

requirements of the project then why 
are these points/clauses removed or 
amended from the RFP 
 
As we understood devices like smart 
screen, speakers and CCTV cameras are 
going to connect this switch, Normally 
CCTV camaras are generating high 
multicast trafic Removing the 
requirement for minimum routing and 
multicast capacities from the switch 
specification will limit the switch’s ability 
to handle large-scale multicast  traffic. 
Without support for at least 512 IPv4 
and IPv6 unicast routes, 512 IGMP 
groups, 512 MLD groups, and sufficient 
ingress entries, the network may 
experience routing limitations, multicast 
traffic drops mans criticle CCTV footages 
, and degraded overall performance. 
 
This can lead to connectivity issues, 
reduced scalability, and potential service 
disruptions, especially in environments 
with multiple high-bandwidth devices or 
complex multicast and routing 
requirements. 
 
Therefore, we strongly request that this 
requirement be included once again in 
the RFP. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

transition from an IPv4-only network to an IPv6-
only network design 
When there are no changes in the 
architechture and performace 
requirements of the project then why 
are these points/clauses removed or 
amended from the RFP 
 
Removing this clause will lead to limit 
the network’s ability to support future-
ready addressing and transition 
strategies. It may create challenges in 
migrating from an IPv4-only network to 
an IPv6-enabled or IPv6-only 
environment, leading to compatibility 
issues with modern applications and 
devices. 
 
This can result in reduced scalability, 
increased complexity in network design, 
and potential additional costs for future 
upgrades or workarounds to support 
IPv6 integration. 
 
Therefore, we strongly request that this 
requirement be included once again in 
the RFP. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

4 Uplink Port 
 2*10/100/1000M and 1*10G 
As per the GFGNL Network Architecture 
guidelines, adequate protection to 
ensure high availability and network 
resilience untill Last mile connectivity of 
GFGNL  the OLT must be equipped with a 
minimum of two (2) 10G uplink 
interfaces to support u OLT uplink is 
required to be protected. The OLT 
should therefore be equipped with a 
minimum of 2 × 10G uplink ports to 
enable protection mechanisms such as 
LAG (Link Aggregation Group) or ERPS 
(Ethernet Ring Protection Switching). 
In the case of a Mini OLT, these uplinks 
should support protection across GP 
Ring / Block Ring / Router connectivity to 
ensure network resilience. 
We understand that 2 × 10/100/1000M 
connectivity can be achieved either 
through an onboard electrical RJ45 port 
or via an electrical SFP interface. 
For better clarity in BOM consideration, 
we request you to please review and 
confirm the following & Modify : 
Day 1 Configuration: OLT to be 
provisioned with uplink optics as below  
1 × 10G (Optical)

the access  GPON side and provide 
network-side protection with sub-50 ms 
restoration using ITU-T G.8032 (ERPS) or 
equivalent mechanisms to ensure high 
availability and service continuity

ERPS or equivalent 
standards

IX. 
Technic
al 
Specific
ations 
for CPE 
(custom
er 
premise 
equipm
ent) L2 
manage
able 
switch – 
3.0 MAC  
Support for minimum 8K MAC entries 
The existing minimum capacity of 8K 
MAC entries may be insufficient to 
support modern enterprise and high-
density network environments. With the 
increasing number of connected devices, 
adoption of virtualization, VLAN 
segmentation, and IoT deployments, the 
MAC table size requirement has 
significantly grown. 
Enhancing the minimum MAC table 
capacity to 16K will: 
 
Ensure improved scalability to 
accommodate network expansion 
Prevent MAC table overflow and 
associated traffic flooding issues 
Maintain optimal switching performance 
in dense deployments 
Support future growth and evolving 
network architecture requirements 
 
Therefore, it is recommended to revise 
the requirement to a minimum of 16K 
MAC entries to align with current and 
future network demands. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

shall adhere to the “STANDARD FOR 
GENERIC REQUIREMENTS TEC 
48050:2024”, instead of referencing 
multiple legacy standards (such as 2016, 
Nov’09, ITU, etc ).

clause as follows: 
 
Router EMS must comply & certified

Generic Requirements).

against the BSNL Master Tender, or 
certification must be completed prior to 
shipment.

Page 39 of

4 Router OEM 
Experience 
 The offered Make-Model for Block Router and GP 
Routers can be from same OEM or different OEM. 
However, the same or its earlier version should 
have been supplied for minimum 30% of required 
quantities for each relevant line item under the 
financial bid Line Items 4(a), 4(b), and 4(c)." 
Allowing different OEMs for Block 
Routers (Type A & Type B separately) 
and GP Routers (Type C & Type D 
separately) is likely to create huge 
operational and interoperability 
challenges. The RFP also permits quoting 
of primary and secondary OEMs for each 
line item, which further increases 
complexity. 
Additionally, as separate EMS are 
required for each OEM, managing 
multiple OEM ecosystems will make 
centralized network management 
extremely difficult and inefficient. 
In view of the above and to ensure 
simplified network operations, better 
interoperability, and ease of 
maintenance, we recommend that both 
Block Routers and GP Routers be 
procured from a single OEM. Further, 
the qualification criteria as defined in the 
tender should uniformly apply. 
Allowing only one 
OEM would be 
restrictive in nature 
and will limit 
competition. To 
ensure wider 
participation and 
competitive 
bidding, the RFP 
provision permitting 
same or different 
OEMs for Block and 
GP Routers stands 
unchanged. 
The bidder is 
further advised that 
overall system 
integration, 
interoperability, 
and centralized 
network 
management shall 
be the responsibility 
of the PIA, and shall 
be ensured in 
compliance with the 
RFP requirements. 
Accordingly, there is 
no change in the 
RFP.

GP routers Type-C 
c. 4 port 1G electrical RJ45(Without SFP) 
A universal port can operate as either an 
electrical (RJ45) interface or an optical 
interface through the use of the 
appropriate transceiver/module, offering 
significantly greater deployment 
flexibility than fixed-type ports. Universal 
ports provide superior port utilization, 
investment protection, simplified 
inventory management, and seamless 
migration between copper and fiber 
connectivity compared to fixed RJ45 
interfaces. This enables service providers 
to adapt to evolving network 
requirements while reducing both CAPEX 
and OPEX. Considering these 
advantages, several national and state-
level BBNL tenders have specified 4 × 
1GE ports with 10 km optical pluggable 
transceivers instead of fixed RJ45 ports, 
thereby ensuring greater deployment 
flexibility and long-term scalability of the 
network infrastructure. 
Request to amend the clause as: 
c. 4 ports of 1G configurable as either 
an electrical (RJ45) interface or an 
optical interface  
Pls read the 
modified RFP clause 
as "4 port 1G (with 
10km Optical or 
Electrical 
pluggable/SFP )" 
instead of "4 port 
electrical 1G  
ethernet (RJ45 
without SFP )" for 
Type-C and "4 port 
1G electrical 
RJ45(Without SFP)" 
for Type-D routers 
specifications

PQC(Post Quantum Cryptography)- Quantum Safe 
feature support

PQC/Quantum Safe feature requirement 
shall be applicable only to the SNOC 
solution and associated security 
infrastructure. Compliance of GP and

security mechanisms like ITSAR standard 
and cryptographic agility, without 
mandating native PQC functionality on 
access and aggregation routers.

participation, promote fair competition, 
and avoid restricting otherwise fully 
compliant MPLS-IP routing solutions that

SFP)

The traffic at each GP is 1Gbps on day 1 
and scalable to 10G. Even if the traffic 
scales to 2Gbps then the total traffic in 
the ring would be 12 Gbps ( 2Gbps x 6 
routers in a ring). So the sub block router 
will need 25 Gbps interfaces to cater to 
this increased traffc. We request you to 
ask for 25G interfaces in addition to the 
asked interfaces.

contract or fails to execute it satisfactorily beyond 
the provisions of Para 4.1 & 4.2.

documents/ information, where  
required.

GFGNL is just and proper to order for banning of 
business dealing with a vendor/ supplier.

i) Banning of business for 3 years which implies

banning order.

Minimu
m

al

tions for 
Uninterr
upted

at GP 
Point No. 28 
        UPS for GP  - Recharge time -6-8 hours 
We wish to submit that the specified 
recharge time of 6–8 hours for Li-ion 
batteries is achievable under ideal 
operating conditions. However, at GP 
(Gram Panchayat) locations, the utility 
power supply is often unstable and 
subject to frequent interruptions and 
voltage fluctuations, which may 
adversely affect the charging process. 
 
Since the system is required to provide 6 
hours of backup at full load, a longer 
recharge duration is essential to ensure 
complete battery charging and reliable 
operation under field conditions. 
 
Therefore, we request you to kindly 
revise the battery recharge time 
specification from 6–8 hours to 8–10 
hours, which would be more practical 
and suitable for actual site conditions 
without affecting system performance 
and availability. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

5.3.1 
c. The handover/ takeover shall be completed 
within a maximum time period as per below table 
(Table X.1.1) from the agreement signed date, in 
“good condition” (optical power is in the range -8 
dBm to -25 dBm). 
b. The handover of the BharatNet Phase-I and 
phase-II network shall be Block wise, for those 
blocks, where at least 50% GPs in Phase-I and 
100% GPs in Phase-II are having optical power as 
specified above. 
c. The PIA shall be responsible for overall O&M of 
the OFC network including GPON equipment, 
logistics, replacement of faulty GPON, SPV 
equipment, spares management and also after the 
MPLS routers are commissioned in the Block and 
GP. Further, Selected PIA shall be responsible to 
improve the OFC network/remaining core of 
phase-I and phase-II on achieving -25dB or better 
optical power from day-1 of the contract signed. 
Considering the handover / takeover 
only on the ONTs where the optical 
power is in the range of -8 dBm to -25 
dBm will only test 1 fiber which is live 
between OLT and ONT. This doesn't 
provide mechanism to tests the 
remaining spare fiber. 
 
As the PIA shall be responsible for overall 
O&M of entire network and meet the 
desired SLAs as per the RFP, it becomes 
important to tests the spare fibers as 
well. 
 
We request you to consider testing of 
the spare fiber and only take over the 
fibers where the optical loss (test 
through OTDR and LSPM) is within the 
standard limits i.e., 0.3 dBm / KM for 
1550 nm and 0.4 dB / KM for 1310 nm. 
This will ensure PIA takes over the 
healthy network which will enable the 
PIA to meet the desired O&M SLAs 
mentioned in the RFP. 
 
Taking over of spare fiber in a healthy 
conditions also helps during project 
execution as the replacement of 
damaged OFC becomes minimal. 
 
Request you to review and consider this. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

c) Each GP ring preferabally integrate in dual 
homed on adjacent block routers with optimized 
OFC route. Where, dual homed means, GP router 
to be parented to two-different sub-blocks for 
mitigating any single point of failure risk associated 
with router/location.

prefer GP ring dual homed i.e., GP ring 
originating from one sub-block / block 
router and terminating it to the adjacent 
sub-block / block router. However, PIA 
shall also plan a GP ring in a single 
homed i.e., GP ring originating and 
terminating to the same block / sub-
block router depending on the desktop 
and physical survey. There are no 
restrictions in planning the GP ring single 
homed.

4.42.6 
Limitation of Liability 
Bidder’s aggregate liability arising out of 
or in connection with the contract, 
whether based on contract, tort, 
statutory warranty or otherwise, shall be 
limited to an aggregate of most recent 
12 months of the charges collected by 
the bidder under the affected order.  The 
Bidder shall not be liable for any special, 
indirect, incidental or consequential 
damages of any kind including but not 
limited to loss of use, data, profit, 
income, business, anticipated savings, 
reputation, and more generally, any loss 
of an economic or financial nature, 
whether these may be deemed as 
consequential or arising directly and 
naturally from the incident giving rise to 
the claim. 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP.

18. 
The project implementation shall follow 
underground (UG) optical fibre cable (OFC). 
Primarily, the PIA will plan for OFC construction 
through underground laying. However, where 
underground laying is not feasible, the preferred 
alternative will be the laying of ADSS (All-dielectric 
Self-Supporting) OFC, preferably utilizing existing 
electricity infrastructure, subject to approval by 
the local distribution company (DISCOM) in rural 
area. 
If existing infrastructure cannot be utilized, new 
pole infrastructure will be installed to support 
ADSS OFC laying. Wherever the existing electricity 
poles are not available; the PIA shall erect a 7 
Meter RCC pole. The PIA shall have to replace such 
poles damaged for any reason whatsoever during 
the contract period without any additional cost to 
GFGNL.

underground (UG) optical fibre cable (OFC). 
Primarily, the PIA will plan for OFC construction 
through underground laying. However, where 
underground laying is not feasible, the preferred 
alternative will be the laying of ADSS (All-
dielectric Self-Supporting) OFC, preferably 
utilizing existing electricity infrastructure, subject 
to approval by the local distribution company 
(DISCOM) in rural area. If existing infrastructure 
cannot be utilized, new pole infrastructure will be 
installed to support ADSS OFC laying. Wherever the 
existing electricity poles are not available; the PIA 
shall erect a 7 Meter RCC pole. The PIA shall have 
to replace such poles damaged for any reason 
whatsoever during the contract period without any 
additional cost to GFGNL. 
Where UG OFC not possible and 
preferred alternative connecteivity using 
ADSS cable to be carried out for GP to GP 
Connectivity , what kind of Aerial 
Accessorise and Fixture to be used  viz. 
Helix Type -I , Helix Type-II or Wedge 
Type -III as per TESEC. Kindly guide.  
Pls refer RFP clause 
- Annexure-D >>

5.18 LAST MILE CONNECTIVITY TO THE VILLAGES ( 
Revenue Village Connectivity) 
a) 
Village connectivity beyond Gram Panchayats (GPs) 
shall be provided on demand for revenue villages 
through aerial linear OFC connectivity from the 
respective GP. For the purpose of this clause, “on 
demand” shall mean those villages where a 
minimum demand of five (5) home fiber 
connections has been registered on the BNU portal 
or as per the information provided by the State.

configuration:

switching capacity of minimum 800 Gbps. The 
router should be enabled on day 1 with the 
following 
configuration: 
a. 4 port of 100G (with 40km /80Km pluggable) 
b. 12 port of 10 G (with 40km pluggable) 
c. 4 port of 1G/10G (All ports 
should be usable for both 1G as well as 10G as per 
plugged SFP) 
(2) Type B (Sub-Block) 
Router should support switching capacity of 
minimum 300 Gbps. 
The router should be enabled on day 
1 with the following configuration: 
2 port of 100G (with 40km / 80Km pluggable) 
8 port of 10G (with 40km pluggable) 
4 port of 1G/10G (All ports should be usable for 
both 1G as well as 10G as per plugged SFP) 
c. Note: Pluggables will be as per 
Fiber planning

Section 
5.1 
5.1 — Scope 
of Work 
(Page 128 of 
RFP) 
 
[General RFP 
Structure — 
Single 
Package] 
Package Structure — 
Division of Single Package 
into Two Packages 
EXISTING PROVISION: 
The entire ABP Phase-III scope — 
covering 34 Districts, 14,229 Gram 
Panchayats and 4,141 Revenue Villages 
across Gujarat — has been structured as 
a Single Package spanning all 6 Zones 
(Ahmedabad, Gandhinagar, Surat, 
Vadodara, Rajkot & Bhavnagar). A single 
implementing agency is expected to 
execute the complete statewide scope 
under one contract. 
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENT: 
It is proposed that the project be divided 
into Two (2) separate Packages / Lots: 
  • Package A — 3 Zones  
  • Package B — 3 Zones 
Each Package to carry independent 
eligibility criteria, Bid Security, and Work 
Order — on the lines of the BharatNet 
Phase-II two-package model successfully 
implemented in Gujarat. 
 
JUSTIFICATION: 
1. The single-package structure 
concentrates the entire statewide scope 
under one agency, severely limiting 
competition to a handful of very large 
entities. 
2. BharatNet Phase-II in Gujarat was 
successfully executed in two packages 
(Package-A: 13 districts; Package-B: 10 
districts). Shivranjani Construction Co, as 
an existing O&M partner of GFGNL since 
Phase-II, affirms the operational 
advantages of the two-package model 
from firsthand experience. 
3. Division into two packages will: (a) 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP. 


Page 410 of 412 
 
attract more qualified bidders; (b) 
reduce execution risk through 
geographical distribution; (c) enable 
faster mobilisation and commissioning; 
and (d) allow capable mid-to-large firms 
— the actual ground-level implementors 
— to meaningfully participate. 
4. A two-package model reduces single-
vendor dependency risk over the 10-year 
O&M period.

Section 
3.1 
3.1 — Pre-
Qualification 
Criteria, 
Table-02, 
Sr. No. A — 
Financial 
Turnover 
(Page 44 of 
RFP) 
Consortium Partner 
Minimum Average Annual 
Turnover — Revision from 
Rs. 400 Cr. to Rs. 100-125 Cr. 
EXISTING PROVISION: 
For Consortium bids (Lead Bidder + one 
or two Consortium Partners), each 
Consortium Partner must have a 
Minimum Average Annual Turnover of 
Rs. 400 Cr. during the last 3 Financial 
Years (FY 2022-23, FY 2023-24, FY 2024-
25). This applies to both (a) Lead + 1 
Consortium Partner and (b) Lead + 2 
Consortium Partners. 
PROPOSED AMENDMENT: 
It is proposed that the minimum Average 
Annual Turnover requirement for 
Consortium Partner(s) be revised to Rs. 
100-125 Cr. (Average Annual Turnover 
during last 3 Financial Years). 
Note: The Lead Bidder's financial 
threshold may remain unchanged as per 
the current RFP. 
JUSTIFICATION: 
1. The Rs. 400 Cr. threshold for each 
Consortium Partner is disproportionately 
high and eliminates a large category of 
technically experienced OFC / Civil / 
Telecom EPC firms from participating as 
partners.2. The overall financial 
robustness of a consortium is primarily 
ensured by the Lead Bidder's threshold. 
Placing an equally steep bar on partners 
defeats the intent of consortium-based 
bidding.3. Shivranjani Construction Co, 
with 7 years of on-ground Phase-II O&M 
experience across Gujarat, has directly 
No change. The 
provision shall 
remain as per the 
RFP. 


Page 412 of 412 
 
S
r
. 
N
o 
Section 
No. 
Clause No. 
Reference/Subject 
Clarification Sought 
GFGNL Clarification 
observed that many of the most skilled 
and reliable OFC / Civil / infrastructure 
firms operate in the Rs. 100–125 Cr. 
annual turnover range. These are the 
firms that actually deliver the work on 
the ground. 
4. Reducing the threshold to Rs. 100-125 
Cr. will: (a) widen the pool of eligible 
consortium partners; (b) allow Lead 
Bidders to form technically stronger 
teams; (c) promote genuine competition; 
and (d) result in better on-ground 
execution by firms with proven domain 
expertise.
